<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every sufficiently large dimension <i>n</i>, we construct a centrally symmetric convex body whose translative covering density exceeds $`c n\log n`$, where <i>c</i> &gt; 0 is absolute. This disproves the existence of a universal linear upper bound and matches the order of Rogers' upper bound.

- **claim-main-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $n_0$ such that, for every integer $n\ge n_0$, some centrally symmetric convex body $K_n\subset\mathbb R^n$ satisfies $$\theta_T(K_n)>c n\log n.$$
  - Explanation: In every sufficiently large dimension there is a centrally symmetric convex body whose translative covering density exceeds a fixed multiple of n log n. No universal linear upper bound can hold, even for symmetric bodies.
- **claim-volume-event** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-volume)): The body in <a href="#eq:body" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:body">[eq:body]</a> satisfies $$\mathbb P\{\|K\|>2v\omega_n a^n\} \ge \frac{\exp(-1)-2v}{3-2v}>0.$$
  - Explanation: The random body built from Poisson slab cuts has volume above a fixed multiple of the ball volume with probability bounded below by a positive constant.
- **claim-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-localization)): For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly for $1\le R\le n^2$, the family $\mathcal F_n$ has the following properties. With $\lambda_0=\log(1+D/a)$, $$\label{eq:pattern-entropy} N_n\le n^{C_1}\exp(\lambda_0n),\qquad \log\|\mathcal F_n\| \le\exp\bigl(\lambda_0n+O(\log n)\bigr),$$ where $C_1$ and the implicit constant depend only on the fixed parameters.* *Let $\mathcal U\subset S^{n-1}$ be any finite set, and put $$K=bB\cap\bigcap_{u\in\mathcal U}\{x:\|u\cdot x\|\le1\},\qquad K^+=(b+3\eta)B\cap \bigcap_{u\in\mathcal U}\{x:\|u\cdot x\|\le1+3\eta\}.$$ Suppose that $\Lambda$ is any full-rank lattice, $T\subset\mathbb R^n$ is finite, and $$\frac{\|T\|}{\det\Lambda}\le \rho_0:=\frac{R}{2v\omega_na^n}.$$ If $K+\Lambda+T$ covers $\mathbb R^n$, there is a list $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_m)\in\mathcal F_n$ such that $$\label{eq:localized-cover} B_D\subseteq\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}(p_j+K^+).$$ The constructed list in fact satisfies $\|G_P\|\ge\|B_D\|/2$.* *For every $P\in\mathcal F_n$ and $y\in G_P$, list all residual vectors $x_i=y-p_j$ with $\|y-p_j\|\le b+3\eta$, retaining their indices, and denote their number by $m_y$. Then $$\label{eq:local-residual-budget} \begin{gathered} r_--2\eta\le \|x_i\|\le b+3\eta,\qquad m_y\le2C_{\mathrm{loc}}R,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^{m_y}w_a(\|x_i\|)\le2C_{\mathrm{loc}}R,\\ m_y+\sum_{i=1}^{m_y}w_a(\|x_i\|)\le4C_{\mathrm{loc}}R. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Any periodic covering of low center intensity yields, after averaging over the period and rounding to a grid, a center list in a fixed finite family that covers the small window. The family has controlled size and every list in it has bounded residual budgets.
- **claim-cap-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-caps)): Fix $1<a<2$ and $A,Q,B_0>0$. There exist constants $C_a,c_a'>0$, $C_{a,A}>0$, and $L=L(a)>0$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $n$, depending on $a,A,Q,B_0$. Let $\sigma$ be normalized surface measure on $S^{n-1}$, and set $$M=\sigma\{u:\|u\cdot e_1\|>1/a\}^{-1},\qquad \mu=M\sigma.$$ Let $x_1,\ldots,x_m\in\mathbb{R}^n$ and $\epsilon_1,\ldots,\epsilon_m\in\mathbb{R}$, where $1\le m\le n^2$, satisfy $$a\left(1-\frac{A\log n}{n}\right)-Qn^{-4} \le \|x_i\|\le a(1+1/n)+Qn^{-4},\qquad \|\epsilon_i\|\le Qn^{-4}.$$ Define $$C_i=\{u\in S^{n-1}:\|u\cdot x_i\|>1+\epsilon_i\},\quad c_i=\mu(C_i),\quad \bar c_i=\min(c_i,1/2),\quad s_i=-\log\bar c_i,$$ and, using projective angles, define $$d(i,j)=\sqrt n\,\angle(x_i,x_j)+\sqrt{\|s_i-s_j\|},\qquad r(S)=\min_{k\in S}\max_{i\in S}d(k,i)^2$$ for each nonempty set $S$ of indices. Then $$\begin{aligned} n^{-C_{a,A}}\le c_i&\le C_a, &s_i&\le C_a\bigl(1+n[1-\|x_i\|^2/a^2]_+\bigr), \label{eq:caps-cost}\\ \frac{\mu(C_i\cap C_j)}{\sqrt{\bar c_i\bar c_j}} &\le C_a\exp\{-c_a'd(i,j)^2\}. \label{eq:caps-affinity} \end{aligned}$$ The function $d$ is a pseudometric on the indexed list. If $r(S)\le B_0\log n$ and $i_0\in S$ minimizes the raw intensity $c_i$, then $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i\in S}C_i\right) \ge e^{-L(1+r(S)+\log\|S\|)}\bar c_{i_0}. \label{eq:caps-lens}$$ In particular, $L$ is independent of $A,Q,B_0$; these parameters may affect the starting dimension.
  - Explanation: Cutting caps of residual vectors in a thin shell have controlled measure and radial cost, two caps overlap with exponentially small affinity in the scaled angle, and a cluster of nearby caps has a common intersection of controlled loss.
- **claim-caps-disjoint** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-caps-disjoint)): With $\varepsilon=2^{-30}$ and $a=1+\varepsilon$, caps satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:caps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:caps">4</a> are disjoint whenever their axes have projective angle greater than $4\sqrt\varepsilon$, for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: For the fixed numerical parameters, cutting caps are exactly disjoint once the projective angle between their axes is large enough, which gives zero overlap rather than just an exponentially small one.
- **claim-block-estimates** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-blocks)): Fix constants $C_0\ge1$, $c_0>0$, and $L\ge1$. There are constants $B_0,K,C\ge1$ and $n_1$, depending only on these three constants, with the following property. Let $n\ge n_1$, let $(X,\mu)$ be an atomless finite measure space, and let $(C_i)_{i\in I}$ be an indexed list of measurable sets with $0<c_i:=\mu(C_i)<\infty$ and $1\le m:=\|I\|\le n^2$. Set $$\bar c_i=\min(c_i,1/2),\qquad s_i=-\log\bar c_i,$$ and let $d$ be a pseudometric on $I$. For nonempty $S\subseteq I$, write $$r(S)=\min_{j\in S}\max_{i\in S}d(i,j)^2,$$ and choose an index $i(S)\in S$ minimizing the uncapped measure $c_i$. Assume that $$\label{eq:block-affinity-input} \frac{\mu(C_i\cap C_j)}{\sqrt{\bar c_i\bar c_j}} \le C_0e^{-c_0d(i,j)^2}\qquad(i,j\in I),$$ and that, whenever $r(S)\le B_0\log n$, $$\label{eq:block-lens-input} \mu\left(\bigcap_{i\in S}C_i\right) \ge e^{-L(1+r(S)+\log\|S\|)}\bar c_{i(S)}.$$ Then there is a partition $\mathcal P$ of $I$ and measurable sets $E_S\subseteq\bigcap_{i\in S}C_i$, $S\in\mathcal P$, of measures $\lambda_S=\mu(E_S)$ such that $$\label{eq:block-anchor-loss} n^{-K}\bar c_{i(S)}\le\lambda_S\le\frac12.$$ Writing $q_S=-\log\lambda_S$ and $$w_{ST}= \frac{\mu(E_S\cap E_T)} {\sqrt{\lambda_S(1+\lambda_S)\lambda_T(1+\lambda_T)}},$$ we have $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{S\in\mathcal P}q_S &\le\sum_{i\in I}s_i+\frac{m}{10}, \label{eq:block-log-cost}\\ \sum_{S\in\mathcal P}\log(1+\lambda_S^{-1}) &\le C\left(m+\sum_{i\in I}s_i\right), \label{eq:block-first-budget}\\ \sum_{S\in\mathcal P} \log\left(1+\sum_{T\in\mathcal P\setminus\{S\}}w_{ST}\right) &\le C\left(m+\sum_{i\in I}s_i\right). \label{eq:block-row-budget} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Given an indexed list of sets with pairwise affinity and common-intersection hypotheses, a merging procedure produces a partition into blocks and a measurable subset inside each block's common intersection. The chosen measures satisfy a polynomial anchor-loss bound, and the total logarithmic cost and overlap-row budgets are linear in the number of sets plus the sum of their costs.
- **claim-finite-janson** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-janson)): Let $(X_v)_{v\in V}$ be independent Bernoulli variables on a finite set $V$, and let $(H_i)_{i=1}^N$ be an indexed family of nonempty subsets of $V$, with repetitions allowed. Set $$I_i=\prod_{v\in H_i}X_v,\qquad Y=\sum_{i=1}^NI_i,\qquad m=\mathbb{E}Y, \qquad \Delta^*=\sum_{\substack{1\le i,j\le N\\H_i\cap H_j\ne\varnothing}} \mathbb{E}(I_iI_j).$$ If $m>0$, then $\Delta^*\ge m$ and $$\mathbb{P}(Y=0)\le \exp\left(-\frac{m^2}{2\Delta^*}\right). \label{eq:finite-janson}$$ The dependency sum is ordered and includes the diagonal.
  - Explanation: For independent Bernoulli variables and an indexed family of subsets with repetitions, the probability that no indexed product occurs is at most an exponential of minus the squared mean over twice the ordered dependency sum, diagonal included.
- **claim-poisson-lower-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-poisson)): Let $\Pi$ be a Poisson process on a finite nonatomic standard Borel measure space. For each member of a finite nonempty family, take a finite nonempty labeled list of measurable slots of positive intensity. Let $Z_\alpha$ count their injective assignments, let $\nu_\alpha$ be the product of their intensities, and define $D_{\alpha\beta}$ by <a href="#eq:diagram-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:diagram-definition">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>. Then $\mathbb{E}Z_\alpha=\nu_\alpha$, and $$\mathbb{P}(Z_\alpha=0\text{ for all }\alpha\in\mathcal A) \le\exp\left[-\frac{(\sum_\alpha\nu_\alpha)^2} {2\sum_{\alpha,\beta}D_{\alpha\beta}}\right]. \label{eq:poisson-janson}$$ The denominator is positive: the full identity matching in $D_{\alpha\alpha}$ contributes $\nu_\alpha$.
  - Explanation: The finite Janson inequality passes to Poisson witnesses on a nonatomic measure space through a cell-partition limit. The probability that every witness count vanishes is bounded by an exponential in terms of total activity and total diagram covariance.
- **claim-matching-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diagrams)): For positive-measure slot lists and the diagrams in <a href="#eq:diagram-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:diagram-definition">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>, the following bounds hold. For one witness type, suppress its label and set $$\delta_i=1+\lambda_i^{-1},\qquad w_{ij}=\frac{\mu(E_i\cap E_j)} {\sqrt{\lambda_i(1+\lambda_i)\lambda_j(1+\lambda_j)}} \quad(i\ne j).$$ Then $$1+\frac{D_{\alpha\alpha}}{\nu_\alpha^2} \le\prod_i\delta_i\prod_i\left(1+\sum_{j\ne i}w_{ij}\right). \label{eq:self-diagram-product}$$ For two witness types, put $$\rho_{\alpha\beta}= \sum_{i\in I_\alpha,j\in I_\beta} \frac{\mu(E_{\alpha i}\cap E_{\beta j})} {\lambda_{\alpha i}\lambda_{\beta j}}.$$ Then $$D_{\alpha\beta}\le\nu_\alpha\nu_\beta (e^{\rho_{\alpha\beta}}-1). \label{eq:cross-diagram-product}$$ In particular, if every cross-slot intersection has measure zero, then $D_{\alpha\beta}=0$.
  - Explanation: The self-diagram sum of a slot list is bounded by a product over slots, and a cross diagram is bounded through the sum of normalized pairwise intersection measures. Cross diagrams vanish when all cross-slot intersections are null.
- **claim-witnesses** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-witnesses)): Fix $A_0>0$. There are constants $A,C_*,H>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, the following holds. Let $R\ge1$, and let $(x_{\alpha i})_{i=1}^{m_\alpha}$, $\alpha\in\mathcal A$, be any finite nonempty family of deterministic lists satisfying $$1\le m_\alpha\le n^2,\qquad r_--3\eta\le \|x_{\alpha i}\|\le b+3\eta.$$ Define $$C_{\alpha i}=\{u\in S^{n-1}:\|u\cdot x_{\alpha i}\|>1+3\eta\}, \qquad s_{\alpha i}=-\log\min\{\mu(C_{\alpha i}),1/2\},$$ and suppose $m_\alpha+\sum_i s_{\alpha i}\le A_0R$. For each list there is a nonempty block partition and measurable slots $E_{\alpha S}\subseteq\bigcap_{i\in S}C_{\alpha i}$, chosen independently of $\Pi$, with positive intensities at most $1/2$. Their activities and diagrams satisfy $$e^{-AR}\le\nu_\alpha\le1,\qquad D_{\alpha\alpha}\le\nu_\alpha^2e^{C_*R}. \label{eq:witness-self}$$ For distinct $\alpha,\beta$, if all cross-residual angles exceed $H\sqrt{\log n/n}$, then $$D_{\alpha\beta}\le n^{-3}\nu_\alpha\nu_\beta. \label{eq:witness-cross}$$ If all those angles exceed $4\sqrt\varepsilon$, then $D_{\alpha\beta}=0$. A positive injection count for a list implies that every cap in that list is hit by $\Pi$.
  - Explanation: For residual lists with bounded cap budgets, block slots chosen independently of the Poisson process have activities bounded below, bounded self-diagrams, small cross diagrams under logarithmic angular separation, and vanishing cross diagrams under a fixed larger separation.
- **claim-cylinder-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder)): Let $n\ge2$, let $D,L>0$, and let $G\subset B_D$ be measurable with $\|G\|\ge\omega_nD^n/4$. Let $Y,Z$ be independent uniform points of $G$, and let $p,q\in\mathbb R^n$. For $0<\xi<\pi/2$, $$\begin{aligned} &\mathbb P\bigl(0<\|Y-p\|\le L,\ 0<\|Z-q\|\le L, \ \angle(Y-p,Z-q)\le\xi\bigr) \notag\\[-2pt] &\hspace{30mm}\le 8\frac{\omega_{n-1}}{\omega_n} \left(\frac{L\sin\xi}{D}\right)^{n-1} \le 8\sqrt n\left(\frac{L\sin\xi}{D}\right)^{n-1}. \label{eq:cylinder} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Two independent uniform points of a large subset of the window have residuals to fixed centers that are nearly parallel only with probability bounded by a cylinder-volume argument.
- **claim-separated-targets** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-targets)): Fix $H>0$ and $k_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $n$, let $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_N)$ be a deterministic list satisfying $$N\le n^{k_0}\exp(\lambda_0n),$$ and let $G\subset B_D\setminus\{p_1,\ldots,p_N\}$ be measurable with $\|G\|\ge\omega_nD^n/4$. There are $M_n$ distinct points $y_1,\ldots,y_{M_n}\in G$ such that:* 1. *no pair with distinct indices is $H\sqrt{\log n/n}$-bad;* 2. *at most a fraction $10f_n$ of the unordered pairs with distinct indices is $4\sqrt\varepsilon$-bad, where $$f_n=8\sqrt n\,n^{2k_0}\exp(2\lambda_0n) \left(\frac{8\sqrt\varepsilon}{D}\right)^{n-1} =\exp\bigl(-\gamma n+O(\log n)\bigr). \label{eq:bad-pair-rate}$$* *The constants and the dimension threshold are uniform in $P$ and $G$. The selected points can be fixed using only $P$ and $G$.
  - Explanation: For any deterministic center list of controlled length and any large good set in the window, one can choose many target points such that no pair is bad at the fine angular scale and only a tiny fraction is bad at the coarse scale. The choice uses only the list and the good set.
- **claim-pattern-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pattern-tail)): There are constants $c_0,C_{\mathrm{tail}}>0$ and $n_0$ such that, whenever $n\ge n_0$ and $1\le R\le c_0n\log n$, every retained pattern $P\in\mathcal F_n$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:localization">3</a> satisfies $$\mathbb P\left(B_D\subseteq\bigcup_{p\in P}(p+K^+)\right) \le \exp\left[-\exp\bigl(\gamma n-C_{\mathrm{tail}}\log n\bigr)\right]. \label{eq:pattern-tail}$$ The constants are independent of the pattern, its repetitions, and $R$.
  - Explanation: Each retained pattern covers the window by translates of the buffered body with doubly exponentially small probability, uniformly over the pattern and the parameter R in the stated range.
- **claim-sharp-order** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-sharp-order)): As $n\to\infty$, each of the four quantities $$\begin{gathered} \sup_{K\in\mathcal K^n}\theta_T(K),\qquad \sup_{K\in\mathcal K^n_{\mathrm{sym}}}\theta_T(K),\\ \sup_{K\in\mathcal K^n}\theta_L(K),\qquad \sup_{K\in\mathcal K^n_{\mathrm{sym}}}\theta_L(K) \end{gathered}$$ has order $n\log n$, with absolute comparison constants.
  - Explanation: The suprema of translative and lattice covering densities, over all convex bodies and over centrally symmetric ones, all have order n log n with absolute constants.
- **claim-rational-polytope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-rational-polytope)): With the same $c$ and $n_0$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, for every $n\ge n_0$ there is a centrally symmetric polytope $P_n\subset\mathbb R^n$ with rational vertices such that $$\theta_T(P_n)>c n\log n.$$
  - Explanation: The same lower bound with the same constants holds for a centrally symmetric polytope with rational vertices, obtained by inner approximation of the successful body while keeping the volume margin.
- **claim-rational-normals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rational)): The bodies in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> can be chosen in the form $$K=bB\cap\bigcap_{j=1}^m\{x:\|u_j\cdot x\|\le1\}, \qquad u_j\in\mathbb Q^n\cap S^{n-1},$$ with $m<\infty$.
  - Explanation: The extremal bodies can be taken as a spherical truncation cut by finitely many slabs with rational unit normals, because the holes and the strict volume test survive small perturbations of the normals.
- **claim-periodization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-periodization)): For every convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, $$\theta_T(K)=\theta_{\mathrm{per}}(K).$$ The same infimum is obtained by replacing the centered cube upper density by centered cube lower density or centered Euclidean-ball lower or upper density. These are equalities of infima over coverings; the corresponding statistics of one covering need not agree.
  - Explanation: The unrestricted translative covering density equals the periodic one, and the value does not change under the cube or ball, lower or upper, density conventions.

      "explanation": "Cutting caps of residual vectors in a thin shell have controlled measure and radial cost, two caps overlap with exponentially small affinity in the scaled angle, and a cluster of nearby caps has a common intersection of controlled loss.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "Fix constants $C_0\\ge1$, $c_0>0$, and $L\\ge1$. There are constants $B_0,K,C\\ge1$ and $n_1$, depending only on these three constants, with the following property. Let $n\\ge n_1$, let $(X,\\mu)$ be an atomless finite measure space, and let $(C_i)_{i\\in I}$ be an indexed list of measurable sets with $0<c_i:=\\mu(C_i)<\\infty$ and $1\\le m:=|I|\\le n^2$. Set $$\\bar c_i=\\min(c_i,1/2),\\qquad s_i=-\\log\\bar c_i,$$ and let $d$ be a pseudometric on $I$. For nonempty $S\\subseteq I$, write $$r(S)=\\min_{j\\in S}\\max_{i\\in S}d(i,j)^2,$$ and choose an index $i(S)\\in S$ minimizing the uncapped measure $c_i$. Assume that $$\\label{eq:block-affinity-input}\n \\frac{\\mu(C_i\\cap C_j)}{\\sqrt{\\bar c_i\\bar c_j}}\n \\le C_0e^{-c_0d(i,j)^2}\\qquad(i,j\\in I),$$ and that, whenever $r(S)\\le B_0\\log n$, $$\\label{eq:block-lens-input}\n \\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i\\right)\n \\ge e^{-L(1+r(S)+\\log|S|)}\\bar c_{i(S)}.$$ Then there is a partition $\\mathcal P$ of $I$ and measurable sets $E_S\\subseteq\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i$, $S\\in\\mathcal P$, of measures $\\lambda_S=\\mu(E_S)$ such that $$\\label{eq:block-anchor-loss}\n n^{-K}\\bar c_{i(S)}\\le\\lambda_S\\le\\frac12.$$ Writing $q_S=-\\log\\lambda_S$ and $$w_{ST}=\n \\frac{\\mu(E_S\\cap E_T)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_S(1+\\lambda_S)\\lambda_T(1+\\lambda_T)}},$$ we have $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}q_S\n &\\le\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i+\\frac{m}{10},\n \\label{eq:block-log-cost}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\\log(1+\\lambda_S^{-1})\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right),\n \\label{eq:block-first-budget}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\n \\log\\left(1+\\sum_{T\\in\\mathcal P\\setminus\\{S\\}}w_{ST}\\right)\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right).\n \\label{eq:block-row-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-092.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-blocks",
          "exact_quote": "Fix constants $C_0\\ge1$, $c_0>0$, and $L\\ge1$. There are constants $B_0,K,C\\ge1$ and $n_1$, depending only on these three constants, with the following property. Let $n\\ge n_1$, let $(X,\\mu)$ be an atomless finite measure space, and let $(C_i)_{i\\in I}$ be an indexed list of measurable sets with $0<c_i:=\\mu(C_i)<\\infty$ and $1\\le m:=|I|\\le n^2$. Set $$\\bar c_i=\\min(c_i,1/2),\\qquad s_i=-\\log\\bar c_i,$$ and let $d$ be a pseudometric on $I$. For nonempty $S\\subseteq I$, write $$r(S)=\\min_{j\\in S}\\max_{i\\in S}d(i,j)^2,$$ and choose an index $i(S)\\in S$ minimizing the uncapped measure $c_i$. Assume that $$\\label{eq:block-affinity-input}\n \\frac{\\mu(C_i\\cap C_j)}{\\sqrt{\\bar c_i\\bar c_j}}\n \\le C_0e^{-c_0d(i,j)^2}\\qquad(i,j\\in I),$$ and that, whenever $r(S)\\le B_0\\log n$, $$\\label{eq:block-lens-input}\n \\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i\\right)\n \\ge e^{-L(1+r(S)+\\log|S|)}\\bar c_{i(S)}.$$ Then there is a partition $\\mathcal P$ of $I$ and measurable sets $E_S\\subseteq\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i$, $S\\in\\mathcal P$, of measures $\\lambda_S=\\mu(E_S)$ such that $$\\label{eq:block-anchor-loss}\n n^{-K}\\bar c_{i(S)}\\le\\lambda_S\\le\\frac12.$$ Writing $q_S=-\\log\\lambda_S$ and $$w_{ST}=\n \\frac{\\mu(E_S\\cap E_T)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_S(1+\\lambda_S)\\lambda_T(1+\\lambda_T)}},$$ we have $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}q_S\n &\\le\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i+\\frac{m}{10},\n \\label{eq:block-log-cost}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\\log(1+\\lambda_S^{-1})\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right),\n \\label{eq:block-first-budget}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\n \\log\\left(1+\\sum_{T\\in\\mathcal P\\setminus\\{S\\}}w_{ST}\\right)\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right).\n \\label{eq:block-row-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      "exact_statement": "Fix constants $C_0\\ge1$, $c_0>0$, and $L\\ge1$. There are constants $B_0,K,C\\ge1$ and $n_1$, depending only on these three constants, with the following property. Let $n\\ge n_1$, let $(X,\\mu)$ be an atomless finite measure space, and let $(C_i)_{i\\in I}$ be an indexed list of measurable sets with $0<c_i:=\\mu(C_i)<\\infty$ and $1\\le m:=|I|\\le n^2$. Set $$\\bar c_i=\\min(c_i,1/2),\\qquad s_i=-\\log\\bar c_i,$$ and let $d$ be a pseudometric on $I$. For nonempty $S\\subseteq I$, write $$r(S)=\\min_{j\\in S}\\max_{i\\in S}d(i,j)^2,$$ and choose an index $i(S)\\in S$ minimizing the uncapped measure $c_i$. Assume that $$\\label{eq:block-affinity-input}\n \\frac{\\mu(C_i\\cap C_j)}{\\sqrt{\\bar c_i\\bar c_j}}\n \\le C_0e^{-c_0d(i,j)^2}\\qquad(i,j\\in I),$$ and that, whenever $r(S)\\le B_0\\log n$, $$\\label{eq:block-lens-input}\n \\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i\\right)\n \\ge e^{-L(1+r(S)+\\log|S|)}\\bar c_{i(S)}.$$ Then there is a partition $\\mathcal P$ of $I$ and measurable sets $E_S\\subseteq\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_i$, $S\\in\\mathcal P$, of measures $\\lambda_S=\\mu(E_S)$ such that $$\\label{eq:block-anchor-loss}\n n^{-K}\\bar c_{i(S)}\\le\\lambda_S\\le\\frac12.$$ Writing $q_S=-\\log\\lambda_S$ and $$w_{ST}=\n \\frac{\\mu(E_S\\cap E_T)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_S(1+\\lambda_S)\\lambda_T(1+\\lambda_T)}},$$ we have $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}q_S\n &\\le\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i+\\frac{m}{10},\n \\label{eq:block-log-cost}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\\log(1+\\lambda_S^{-1})\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right),\n \\label{eq:block-first-budget}\\\\\n \\sum_{S\\in\\mathcal P}\n \\log\\left(1+\\sum_{T\\in\\mathcal P\\setminus\\{S\\}}w_{ST}\\right)\n &\\le C\\left(m+\\sum_{i\\in I}s_i\\right).\n \\label{eq:block-row-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
      "explanation": "Given an indexed list of sets with pairwise affinity and common-intersection hypotheses, a merging procedure produces a partition into blocks and a measurable subset inside each block's common intersection.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-janson",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X_v)_{v\\in V}$ be independent Bernoulli variables on a finite set $V$, and let $(H_i)_{i=1}^N$ be an indexed family of nonempty subsets of $V$, with repetitions allowed. Set $$I_i=\\prod_{v\\in H_i}X_v,\\qquad Y=\\sum_{i=1}^NI_i,\\qquad m=\\mathbb{E}Y,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta^*=\\sum_{\\substack{1\\le i,j\\le N\\\\H_i\\cap H_j\\ne\\varnothing}}\n                  \\mathbb{E}(I_iI_j).$$ If $m>0$, then $\\Delta^*\\ge m$ and $$\\mathbb{P}(Y=0)\\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{m^2}{2\\Delta^*}\\right).\n \\label{eq:finite-janson}$$ The dependency sum is ordered and includes the diagonal.",
        "source_id": "PM-092.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-janson",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X_v)_{v\\in V}$ be independent Bernoulli variables on a finite set $V$, and let $(H_i)_{i=1}^N$ be an indexed family of nonempty subsets of $V$, with repetitions allowed. Set $$I_i=\\prod_{v\\in H_i}X_v,\\qquad Y=\\sum_{i=1}^NI_i,\\qquad m=\\mathbb{E}Y,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta^*=\\sum_{\\substack{1\\le i,j\\le N\\\\H_i\\cap H_j\\ne\\varnothing}}\n                  \\mathbb{E}(I_iI_j).$$ If $m>0$, then $\\Delta^*\\ge m$ and $$\\mathbb{P}(Y=0)\\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{m^2}{2\\Delta^*}\\right).\n \\label{eq:finite-janson}$$ The dependency sum is ordered and includes the diagonal.",
          "source_id": "PM-092.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(X_v)_{v\\in V}$ be independent Bernoulli variables on a finite set $V$, and let $(H_i)_{i=1}^N$ be an indexed family of nonempty subsets of $V$, with repetitions allowed. Set $$I_i=\\prod_{v\\in H_i}X_v,\\qquad Y=\\sum_{i=1}^NI_i,\\qquad m=\\mathbb{E}Y,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta^*=\\sum_{\\substack{1\\le i,j\\le N\\\\H_i\\cap H_j\\ne\\varnothing}}\n                  \\mathbb{E}(I_iI_j).$$ If $m>0$, then $\\Delta^*\\ge m$ and $$\\mathbb{P}(Y=0)\\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{m^2}{2\\Delta^*}\\right).\n \\label{eq:finite-janson}$$ The dependency sum is ordered and includes the diagonal.",
      "explanation": "For independent Bernoulli variables and an indexed family of subsets with repetitions, the probability that no indexed product occurs is at most an exponential of minus the squared mean over twice the ordered dependency sum, diagonal included.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "kind": "bound",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-poisson",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be a Poisson process on a finite nonatomic standard Borel measure space. For each member of a finite nonempty family, take a finite nonempty labeled list of measurable slots of positive intensity. Let $Z_\\alpha$ count their injective assignments, let $\\nu_\\alpha$ be the product of their intensities, and define $D_{\\alpha\\beta}$ by <a href=\"#eq:diagram-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagram-definition\">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>. Then $\\mathbb{E}Z_\\alpha=\\nu_\\alpha$, and $$\\mathbb{P}(Z_\\alpha=0\\text{ for all }\\alpha\\in\\mathcal A)\n \\le\\exp\\left[-\\frac{(\\sum_\\alpha\\nu_\\alpha)^2}\n                        {2\\sum_{\\alpha,\\beta}D_{\\alpha\\beta}}\\right].\n \\label{eq:poisson-janson}$$ The denominator is positive: the full identity matching in $D_{\\alpha\\alpha}$ contributes $\\nu_\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-092.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-poisson",

Let $Z_\\alpha$ count their injective assignments, let $\\nu_\\alpha$ be the product of their intensities, and define $D_{\\alpha\\beta}$ by <a href=\"#eq:diagram-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagram-definition\">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>. Then $\\mathbb{E}Z_\\alpha=\\nu_\\alpha$, and $$\\mathbb{P}(Z_\\alpha=0\\text{ for all }\\alpha\\in\\mathcal A)\n \\le\\exp\\left[-\\frac{(\\sum_\\alpha\\nu_\\alpha)^2}\n                        {2\\sum_{\\alpha,\\beta}D_{\\alpha\\beta}}\\right].\n \\label{eq:poisson-janson}$$ The denominator is positive: the full identity matching in $D_{\\alpha\\alpha}$ contributes $\\nu_\\alpha$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Pi$ be a Poisson process on a finite nonatomic standard Borel measure space. Then $\\mathbb{E}Z_\\alpha=\\nu_\\alpha$, and $$\\mathbb{P}(Z_\\alpha=0\\text{ for all }\\alpha\\in\\mathcal A)\n \\le\\exp\\left[-\\frac{(\\sum_\\alpha\\nu_\\alpha)^2}\n                        {2\\sum_{\\alpha,\\beta}D_{\\alpha\\beta}}\\right].\n \\label{eq:poisson-janson}$$ The denominator is positive: the full identity matching in $D_{\\alpha\\alpha}$ contributes $\\nu_\\alpha$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagrams",
        "exact_quote": "For positive-measure slot lists and the diagrams in <a href=\"#eq:diagram-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagram-definition\">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>, the following bounds hold. For one witness type, suppress its label and set $$\\delta_i=1+\\lambda_i^{-1},\\qquad\n w_{ij}=\\frac{\\mu(E_i\\cap E_j)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_i(1+\\lambda_i)\\lambda_j(1+\\lambda_j)}}\n \\quad(i\\ne j).$$ Then $$1+\\frac{D_{\\alpha\\alpha}}{\\nu_\\alpha^2}\n \\le\\prod_i\\delta_i\\prod_i\\left(1+\\sum_{j\\ne i}w_{ij}\\right).\n \\label{eq:self-diagram-product}$$ For two witness types, put $$\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}=\n \\sum_{i\\in I_\\alpha,j\\in I_\\beta}\n \\frac{\\mu(E_{\\alpha i}\\cap E_{\\beta j})}\n      {\\lambda_{\\alpha i}\\lambda_{\\beta j}}.$$ Then $$D_{\\alpha\\beta}\\le\\nu_\\alpha\\nu_\\beta\n                         (e^{\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}}-1).\n \\label{eq:cross-diagram-product}$$ In particular, if every cross-slot intersection has measure zero, then $D_{\\alpha\\beta}=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-092.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagrams",
          "exact_quote": "For positive-measure slot lists and the diagrams in <a href=\"#eq:diagram-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagram-definition\">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>, the following bounds hold. For one witness type, suppress its label and set $$\\delta_i=1+\\lambda_i^{-1},\\qquad\n w_{ij}=\\frac{\\mu(E_i\\cap E_j)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_i(1+\\lambda_i)\\lambda_j(1+\\lambda_j)}}\n \\quad(i\\ne j).$$ Then $$1+\\frac{D_{\\alpha\\alpha}}{\\nu_\\alpha^2}\n \\le\\prod_i\\delta_i\\prod_i\\left(1+\\sum_{j\\ne i}w_{ij}\\right).\n \\label{eq:self-diagram-product}$$ For two witness types, put $$\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}=\n \\sum_{i\\in I_\\alpha,j\\in I_\\beta}\n \\frac{\\mu(E_{\\alpha i}\\cap E_{\\beta j})}\n      {\\lambda_{\\alpha i}\\lambda_{\\beta j}}.$$ Then $$D_{\\alpha\\beta}\\le\\nu_\\alpha\\nu_\\beta\n                         (e^{\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}}-1).\n \\label{eq:cross-diagram-product}$$ In particular, if every cross-slot intersection has measure zero, then $D_{\\alpha\\beta}=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-092.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For positive-measure slot lists and the diagrams in <a href=\"#eq:diagram-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:diagram-definition\">[eq:diagram-definition]</a>, the following bounds hold. For one witness type, suppress its label and set $$\\delta_i=1+\\lambda_i^{-1},\\qquad\n w_{ij}=\\frac{\\mu(E_i\\cap E_j)}\n {\\sqrt{\\lambda_i(1+\\lambda_i)\\lambda_j(1+\\lambda_j)}}\n \\quad(i\\ne j).$$ Then $$1+\\frac{D_{\\alpha\\alpha}}{\\nu_\\alpha^2}\n \\le\\prod_i\\delta_i\\prod_i\\left(1+\\sum_{j\\ne i}w_{ij}\\right).\n \\label{eq:self-diagram-product}$$ For two witness types, put $$\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}=\n \\sum_{i\\in I_\\alpha,j\\in I_\\beta}\n \\frac{\\mu(E_{\\alpha i}\\cap E_{\\beta j})}\n      {\\lambda_{\\alpha i}\\lambda_{\\beta j}}.$$ Then $$D_{\\alpha\\beta}\\le\\nu_\\alpha\\nu_\\beta\n                         (e^{\\rho_{\\alpha\\beta}}-1).\n \\label{eq:cross-diagram-product}$$ In particular, if every cross-slot intersection has measure zero, then $D_{\\alpha\\beta}=0$.",
      "explanation": "The self-diagram sum of a slot list is bounded by a product over slots, and a cross diagram is bounded through the sum of normalized pairwise intersection measures. Cross diagrams vanish when all cross-slot intersections are null.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-witnesses",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $A_0>0$. There are constants $A,C_*,H>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, the following holds. Let $R\\ge1$, and let $(x_{\\alpha i})_{i=1}^{m_\\alpha}$, $\\alpha\\in\\mathcal A$, be any finite nonempty family of deterministic lists satisfying $$1\\le m_\\alpha\\le n^2,\\qquad\n r_--3\\eta\\le |x_{\\alpha i}|\\le b+3\\eta.$$ Define $$C_{\\alpha i}=\\{u\\in S^{n-1}:|u\\cdot x_{\\alpha i}|>1+3\\eta\\},\n \\qquad\n s_{\\alpha i}=-\\log\\min\\{\\mu(C_{\\alpha i}),1/2\\},$$ and suppose $m_\\alpha+\\sum_i s_{\\alpha i}\\le A_0R$. For each list there is a nonempty block partition and measurable slots $E_{\\alpha S}\\subseteq\\bigcap_{i\\in S}C_{\\alpha i}$, chosen independently of $\\Pi$, with positive intensities at most $1/2$. Their activities and diagrams satisfy $$e^{-AR}\\le\\nu_\\alpha\\le1,\\qquad\n D_{\\alpha\\alpha}\\le\\nu_\\alpha^2e^{C_*R}.\n \\label{eq:witness-self}$$ For distinct $\\alpha,\\beta$, if all cross-residual angles exceed $H\\sqrt{\\log n/n}$, then $$D_{\\alpha\\beta}\\le n^{-3}\\nu_\\alpha\\nu_\\beta.\n \\label{eq:witness-cross}$$ If all those angles exceed $4\\sqrt\\varepsilon$, then $D_{\\alpha\\beta}=0$. A positive injection count for a list implies that every cap in that list is hit by $\\Pi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-092.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-witnesses",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $A_0>0$. There are constants $A,C_*,H>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, the following holds. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **convex body** ([source](#eq:unrestricted-density)): A convex body in $\mathbb R^n$ is a compact convex set with nonempty interior.
- **locally finite set** ([source](#eq:unrestricted-density)): A set $X\subset\mathbb R^n$ is *locally finite* if every bounded set contains only finitely many of its points.
- **translative covering** ([source](#eq:unrestricted-density)): It gives a *translative covering* of $K$ when $K+X=\mathbb R^n$.
- **cube Q_R** ([source](#eq:unrestricted-density)): Write $Q_R=[-R,R]^n$.
- **translative covering density** ([source](#eq:unrestricted-density)): We define the translative covering density by $$\label{eq:unrestricted-density} \theta_T(K)=\inf_{\substack{X\text{ locally finite}\\K+X=\mathbb R^n}} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K)\limsup_{R\to\infty} \frac{\#(X\cap Q_R)}{(2R)^n}.$$
- **periodic translative covering** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *periodic translative covering* is a covering $$\mathbb R^n=K+\Lambda+T,$$ where $\Lambda$ is a full-rank lattice and $T$ is a finite set of distinct representatives modulo $\Lambda$.
- **center intensity and periodic density** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Its center intensity is $\|T\|/\det\Lambda$, and its density is $\|T\|\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K)/\det\Lambda$.
- **periodic covering density** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Put $$\theta_{\mathrm{per}}(K)=\inf\left\{ \frac{\|T\|\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K)}{\det\Lambda}:K+\Lambda+T=\mathbb R^n \right\}.$$
- **Euclidean ball and volume notation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): We write $\|x\|$ for Euclidean norm, $B_r=\{x\in\mathbb R^n:\|x\|\le r\}$, $B=B_1$, and $\omega_n=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(B)$.
- **projective angle** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For nonzero vectors we use the projective angle $$\angle(x,z)=\arccos\frac{\|x\cdot z\|}{\|x\|\|z\|}\in[0,\pi/2].$$
- **numerical constants of the construction** ([source](#eq:fixed-constants)): Fix the numerical constants $$\label{eq:fixed-constants} \varepsilon=2^{-30},\qquad a=1+\varepsilon,\qquad D=2^{-10}=\varepsilon^{1/3},\qquad v=\frac1{64}.$$
- **Poisson process of slab normals** ([source](#eq:poisson-measure)): and let $\Pi$ be a Poisson process on $S^{n-1}$ with intensity $\mu$.
- **localization constants** ([source](#eq:local-grid)): Define $$C_{\mathrm{loc}}=\frac{1000}{v},\qquad w_a(r)=1+n[1-r^2/a^2]_+ \quad (r\ge0), \qquad h_n=\frac{\eta}{100n}.$$
- **ordered center list** ([source](#eq:local-grid)): An ordered list $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_m)$ over $\mathcal Q_n$ may contain repeated entries.
- **good set of a pattern** ([source](#eq:local-grid)): Define $G_P\subseteq B_D$ to consist of the points $y$ satisfying the following three conditions:
- **cutting caps** ([source](#sec:caps)): The set $$C(x)=\{u\in S^{n-1}:\|u\cdot x\|>1+3\eta\}$$ consists of the normals whose slabs exclude $y$ from $p+K^+$.
- **activity of a witness type** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Define its *activity* by $$\nu_\alpha=\prod_{i\in I_\alpha}\lambda_{\alpha i},$$ and let $Z_\alpha$ count injective assignments of Poisson points to the slots, with the point assigned to $i$ lying in $E_{\alpha i}$.
- **partial matching** ([source](#eq:diagram-definition)): A *partial matching* between $I_\alpha$ and $I_\beta$ is a set of pairs $(i,j)$ with no repeated first or second coordinate.
- **relevant residual list** ([source](#pm-h-23)): $$ Indeed, restrict the integral to $\|t\|\le n^{-1/2}$ and use $\log(1-1/n)\ge-1/(n-1)$. In particular, $\omega_{n-1}/\omega_n\le\sqrt n$. ◻ </details> For a finite center list $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_N)$ and a point $y$ different from its entries, write $$\mathcal R_P(y)=(y-p_i:\ \|y-p_i\|\le b+3\eta)$$ for its labeled list of relevant residuals.
- **xi-bad pair** ([source](#pm-h-23)): For $\xi>0$, call a pair $(y,z)$ *$\xi$-bad* if some residual in $\mathcal R_P(y)$ and some residual in $\mathcal R_P(z)$ have projective angle at most $\xi$.
- **single-lattice covering density** ([source](#pm-h-26)): For a convex body $K$, define its single-lattice covering density by $$\theta_L(K)=\inf_{\Lambda:\ K+\Lambda=\mathbb R^n} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K)}{\det\Lambda}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-rogers-bound | Rogers upper bound on periodic translative covering density | ≤ | $n\log n+n\log\log n+5n$ | Dimension at least three. |
| q-main-lower-bound | Translative covering density of the constructed symmetric body | > | $c n\log n$ | Absolute constant c and dimension threshold n_0; neither is optimized or given an effective value. |
| q-volume-probability | Probability that the random body has large volume | ≥ | $\frac{\exp(-1)-2v}{3-2v}$ | Fixed numerical constants of the construction. |
| q-slack-eps | Numerical slack parameter | = | $2^{-30}$ | Fixed, independent of dimension. |
| q-window-radius | Window radius D | = | $2^{-10}$ | Fixed, independent of dimension. |
| q-volume-fraction | Volume fraction v | = | $\frac1{64}$ | Fixed, independent of dimension. |
| q-rounding-buffer | Buffer eta | = | $n^{-4}$ | Sufficiently large n. |
| q-outer-radius | Outer radius b | = | $a(1+1/n)$ | Sufficiently large n. |
| q-inner-residual-radius | Inner residual radius r_- | = | $a(1-10\log n/n)$ | Sufficiently large n. |
| q-entropy-rate | Pattern growth rate lambda_0 | = | $\log(1+D/a)$ | Fixed, independent of dimension. |
| q-tail-rate-gamma | Tail rate gamma | = | $\log4-2\lambda_0$ | Fixed, independent of dimension. |
| q-gamma-exceeds-lambda | Gamma relative to lambda_0 | > | $3\lambda_0$ | Fixed constants. |
| q-intensity-threshold | Center intensity threshold rho_0 | = | $\frac{R}{2v\omega_n a^n}$ | Positive parameter R. |
| q-localization-constant | Localization constant C_loc | = | $\frac{1000}{v}$ | Fixed. |
| q-rounding-displacement | Rounding displacement delta_n | ≤ | $\frac\eta{200}$ | Every n. |
| q-pattern-list-length | Maximum pattern list length N_n | ≤ | $n^{C_1}\exp(\lambda_0n)$ | Sufficiently large n and R in the stated range. |
| q-pattern-family-entropy | Logarithm of the size of the pattern family | ≤ | $\exp\bigl(\lambda_0n+O(\log n)\bigr)$ | Implicit constant depends only on fixed parameters. |
| q-witness-activity-lower | Witness activity | ≥ | $e^{-AR}$ | Sufficiently large n and R at least one. |
| q-self-diagram | Self-diagram covariance | ≤ | $\nu_\alpha^2e^{C_*R}$ | Sufficiently large n and R at least one. |
| q-cross-diagram | Cross-diagram covariance under angular separation | ≤ | $n^{-3}\nu_\alpha\nu_\beta$ | Distinct types alpha and beta. |
| q-cylinder-probability | Probability of nearly parallel residuals | ≤ | $8\sqrt n\left(\frac{L\sin\xi}{D}\right)^{n-1}$ | Angle xi in the stated range. |
| q-target-count | Number of selected targets | ≤ | $\exp\left(\frac{n\log n}{16}\right)$ | Defined through a floor of this expression. |
| q-bad-pair-rate | Fraction of coarse-bad pairs f_n | = | $\exp\bigl(-\gamma n+O(\log n)\bigr)$ | Dimension tending to infinity. |
| q-pattern-tail | Probability that one pattern covers the window | ≤ | $\exp\left[-\exp\bigl(\gamma n-C_{\mathrm{tail}}\log n\bigr)\right]$ | Uniform over patterns and R in range. |
| q-density-constant-choice | Choice of the density constant c | ≤ | $\frac1{64\max(C_*,1)}$ | Positive rational, independent of dimension. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-volume): Bound the expected volume from below by integrating a survival probability, bound the volume from above by the outer ball, then apply a two-sided mean argument. -> Positive probability of large body volume
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-prop-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-localization): Average over the full multiset of centers, apply Markov inequalities to find a good window, round to a grid, check the three conditions, and count the lists. -> Rounded list in the finite family covering the window
- **step-caps** [claim](#stmt-lem-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-caps): One-dimensional cap estimates, a bisector argument for overlaps, and a tangent concentration argument for common intersections. -> Cost, affinity and common-lens bounds
- **step-caps-disjoint** [claim](#stmt-cor-caps-disjoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-caps-disjoint): Bound the angular radius of each half cap and apply the spherical triangle inequality. -> Zero overlap beyond a fixed angle
- **step-blocks** [claim](#stmt-prop-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-blocks): Merge blocks while the radius and loss conditions hold, then control overlap rows by binning and a terminality count. -> Partition, chosen sets and the three budget bounds
- **step-finite-janson** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-janson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-janson): Harris correlation inequality gives a first bound, then a private thinning variable optimizes the exponent. -> Finite lower-tail inequality
- **step-poisson** [claim](#stmt-prop-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-poisson): Partition space into small cells, apply the finite inequality to cell-occupation indicators, and pass to the limit. -> Poisson lower-tail bound
- **step-diagrams** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagrams) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagrams): Count directed-graph incidences for the self sum and drop matching restrictions for the cross sum. -> Self and cross diagram bounds
- **step-witnesses** [claim](#stmt-cor-witnesses) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-witnesses): Feed the cap estimates into the block construction and the diagram bounds, then choose the angular constant. -> Activity, self and cross diagram bounds for residual lists
- **step-cylinder** [claim](#stmt-lem-cylinder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cylinder): Condition on one point and bound the volume of the cylinder around the line it defines. -> Nearly-parallel probability bound
- **step-targets** [claim](#stmt-lem-targets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-targets): Sample targets at random, union bound over center labels, and apply Markov to the coarse-bad fraction. -> Separated target list
- **step-pattern-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-pattern-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pattern-tail): Select targets, bin their activities, and apply the Poisson lower-tail bound on the largest bin. -> Doubly exponential failure bound for one pattern
- **step-periodization** [claim](#stmt-prop-periodization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-periodization): Periodize finite windows of a covering for the lower bound and compare cell counts for periodic competitors. -> Equality of the two densities and convention invariance
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pattern-tail-2): Union bound over the finite pattern family, pick a realization with large volume and no covering pattern, and transfer through periodization. -> Lower bound for the translative covering density
- **step-sharp-order** [claim](#stmt-cor-sharp-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-sharp-order): Combine the lower bound with the translative and lattice upper bounds. -> Order n log n for all four suprema
- **step-rational-polytope** [claim](#stmt-cor-rational-polytope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rational-polytope): Inner approximation by rational symmetric polytopes with continuity of volume. -> Rational polytope with the same lower bound
- **step-rational** [claim](#stmt-prop-rational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rational): Perturb the normals to rational ones while preserving strict hole inequalities and the strict volume test. -> Body with rational slab normals

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-lower-bound**: For every sufficiently large dimension there is a centrally symmetric convex body whose translative covering density exceeds a fixed absolute multiple of n log n.
- **inv-periodization-equality**: For every convex body the unrestricted translative covering density equals the periodic covering density, so a lower bound for periodic coverings transfers to all coverings.
- **inv-pattern-tail**: Each retained finite center pattern covers the small window with doubly exponentially small probability, uniformly over the finite pattern class.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension**: Applying the n log n lower bound to coverings in small fixed dimensions such as the plane or three-space. -> The theorem only asserts existence of bad bodies beyond an unspecified dimension threshold; it says nothing about low dimensions.
- **anti-euclidean-balls**: Treating the result as a superlinear lower bound for covering by Euclidean balls or other fixed standard bodies. -> The result is a statement about a specially constructed random body; the paper itself leaves the ball case open.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are probabilistic existence statements: the body comes from a random slab process and is fixed by a choice among realizations, with no finite procedure computing it. The rational-normal variant is explicitly described as a finite mathematical object that carries no algorithm for deciding the volume comparisons and no effective bound for the first admissible dimension, so there is no finite constructive output to execute or test.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of bodies and disclaims any procedure for finding them, so there is no routine to implement as a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The density lower bound holds only for every integer n at or above an unspecified threshold, so it constrains no fixed low-dimensional simulation or game world. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No measurable output exists to build a checker or calculator around: the constants are non-explicit and the body is a random realisation. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is a probabilistic existence proof with a union bound over a finite pattern class; the target selection and ordering of rational normal lists are mathematical choices, not an effective procedure. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Indexed lists with retained multiplicities are bookkeeping for the proof, not a storage or lookup structure with operations to support. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper is a dataflow or kernel; the high-dimensional measure estimates have no hardware realisation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Lattices enter only as periodic covering arrangements for convex-body density; no hardness assumption, primitive or security reduction is involved. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no learning model, loss or generalisation claim; its tail bounds concern a Poisson slab process. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The parameter R is only a threshold in a proof by contradiction; no objective is optimised and the constant in the theorem is explicitly not optimised. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no nodes, messages or failure models; density is a purely geometric asymptotic quantity of a covering arrangement. |
| other (formal mathematics specification) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The statements are precise and fully quantified (absolute constants, centrally symmetric bodies, rational-vertex polytopes), so they can serve as targets for formal specification of covering densities; this is an inspirational adaptation and the paper gives no construction to execute. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-large-dimension-threshold** (hypothesis): The main lower bound holds only for every integer n at or above an unspecified threshold n_0; no explicit value of n_0 is given and nothing is claimed for smaller dimensions. Blocked use: ['Applying the n log n lower bound to covering in the plane, three-space or any fixed low dimension', 'Using a concrete numeric dimension cutoff taken from the paper']
- **limit-existential-body** (nonconstructive): The extremal body is obtained as a realization of a random slab process chosen by a probabilistic argument, so the theorem asserts existence only and supplies no procedure to produce the body. Blocked use: ['Presenting the body as the output of an effective generator', 'Claiming a specific constructed body for a given dimension']
- **limit-absolute-constant-unoptimized** (approximation): The constant c in the lower bound is absolute but is not optimized, and it is fixed through a choice depending on an unspecified self-diagram constant, so it carries no sharpness claim. Blocked use: ['Quoting a numeric value of c as the best known constant', 'Deducing a tight quantitative covering density for a particular dimension']
- **limit-symmetric-convex-bodies-only** (formal_scope): The lower bound is an existence statement for a specially constructed centrally symmetric convex body; it is not a statement about prescribed bodies such as Euclidean balls, for which the gap remains. Blocked use: ['Treating the result as a superlinear lower bound for covering by Euclidean balls', 'Applying it to a fixed standard body such as a cube']
- **limit-localization-range-of-R** (hypothesis): The localization step and the finite pattern family are valid only for n sufficiently large and uniformly for R in the range from one up to n squared; the pattern tail bound further needs R at most a small multiple of n log n. Blocked use: ['Using the pattern-count estimates for density parameters outside the stated range', 'Extending the tail bound to R of larger order than n log n']
- **limit-cap-estimates-thin-shell** (hypothesis): The cap overlap and common intersection estimates require radii in a thin shell around a, with the shell, perturbation and block parameters fixed in advance and the starting dimension depending on them. Blocked use: ['Applying the cap affinity bound to residuals outside the thin shell', 'Using the common-intersection bound when the cluster radius exceeds the stated logarithmic cutoff']
- **limit-block-merging-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The block construction needs an atomless finite measure space, the pairwise affinity bound, the common intersection lower bound, and a list of at most n squared sets; without these the partition and chosen subsets are not guaranteed. Blocked use: ['Using the budgets for lists longer than n squared', 'Applying the construction on measure spaces with atoms']
- **limit-poisson-janson-positivity** (hypothesis): The Poisson lower-tail bound is stated for a finite nonatomic standard Borel measure space with slots of positive intensity; the exponent is meaningful only because the denominator is positive. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to atomic measures or slots of zero intensity', 'Using it as a two-sided concentration estimate']
- **limit-target-separation-uniformity** (edge_case): The separated target selection needs a good set of at least a quarter of the window volume and a center list of controlled length, and the cylinder estimate needs the angle strictly between zero and a right angle. Blocked use: ['Selecting separated targets when the good set is smaller than the stated fraction', 'Using the cylinder bound for angles outside the stated range']
- **limit-no-effective-algorithm** (nonconstructive): The rational normal list is specified as a finite mathematical object, but the paper asserts no algorithm for deciding the volume comparisons and no effective bound for the first admissible dimension. Blocked use: ['Implementing a search procedure for the rational-normal body with a running time guarantee', 'Computing the dimension threshold from the proof']
- **limit-density-is-infimum-statistic** (formal_scope): Density equalities are equalities of infima over coverings; the lower and upper densities of an individual nonperiodic arrangement need not agree. Blocked use: ['Reading the density of a single covering as a limit', 'Using periodization to claim an individual covering can be repeated at its original density']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 092 (`lean/docs/092.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A single-lattice covering bound of order $n\log n$](../../preprints/A-single-lattice-covering-bound-of-order-n-log-n-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Translative covering densities of order $n\log n$](../../preprints/Translative-covering-densities-of-order-n-log-n-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives an absolute constant $C>0$ such that every convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge2$, admits a covering by translates along one full-rank lattice $L$ with density $|K|/\mathrm{covol}(L)\le Cn\log n$. The covering is exact, and no symmetry, boundary regularity, or volume normalization is assumed.
>
> The formalization determines the order of the largest covering density in dimension $n$. For all sufficiently large $n$, the suprema of translative covering density and lattice covering density are each bounded above and below by absolute positive multiples of $n\log n$, both over all convex bodies and over centrally symmetric convex bodies.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CoveringOrder
open Set MeasureTheory
open scoped ENNReal

abbrev Space (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)

/-- Actual discrete full-rank lattice coverings; no assumed density theorem. -/
structure LatticeCover {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) where
  lattice : Submodule ℤ (Space n)
  discrete : DiscreteTopology lattice
  fullrank : IsZLattice ℝ lattice
  covers : ∀ y, ∃ l : lattice, y - l.val ∈ K

noncomputable def euclideanVolume (n : ℕ) :
    @Measure (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))
      (@WithLp.measurableSpace 2 (Fin n → ℝ)
        (@MeasurableSpace.pi (Fin n) (fun _ => ℝ) (fun _ => Real.measurableSpace))) :=
  @Measure.map (Fin n → ℝ) (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))
    (@MeasurableSpace.pi (Fin n) (fun _ => ℝ) (fun _ => Real.measurableSpace))
    (@WithLp.measurableSpace 2 (Fin n → ℝ)
      (@MeasurableSpace.pi (Fin n) (fun _ => ℝ) (fun _ => Real.measurableSpace)))
    (@WithLp.toLp 2 (Fin n → ℝ))
    (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => @volume ℝ Real.measureSpace))

noncomputable def euclideanCovolume {n : ℕ}
    (lattice : Submodule ℤ (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  addCovolume lattice (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)) (euclideanVolume n)

noncomputable def latticeDensity {n : ℕ} {K : Set (Space n)}
    (cover : LatticeCover K) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  euclideanVolume n K / euclideanCovolume cover.lattice

noncomputable def thetaL {n : ℕ} (K : Set (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨅ cover : LatticeCover K, latticeDensity cover

end CoveringOrder

namespace TranslativeCovering

open Set Filter MeasureTheory
open scoped ENNReal

abbrev Space (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)

def cube (n : ℕ) (R : ℝ) : Set (Space n) :=
  {x | ∀ i, -R ≤ x i ∧ x i ≤ R}

def LocallyFiniteCenters {n : ℕ} (X : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  ∀ B : Set (Space n), Bornology.IsBounded B → (X ∩ B).Finite

def Covers {n : ℕ} (K X : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  ∀ y, ∃ x ∈ X, y - x ∈ K

def ConvexBody {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  IsCompact K ∧ Convex ℝ K ∧ (interior K).Nonempty

def CentrallySymmetric {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  ∃ z, ∀ x, x ∈ K ↔ (2 : ℝ) • z - x ∈ K

noncomputable def upperCenterIntensity {n : ℕ} (X : Set (Space n)) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  Filter.limsup (fun R : ℝ =>
    (↑(X ∩ cube n R).ncard : ℝ≥0∞) / ENNReal.ofReal ((2 * R) ^ n)) atTop

noncomputable def thetaT {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨅ X : {X : Set (Space n) // LocallyFiniteCenters X ∧ Covers K X},
    volume K * upperCenterIntensity X.val

end TranslativeCovering

namespace CoveringOrder
open Set MeasureTheory
open scoped ENNReal

abbrev Body (n : ℕ) := {K : Set (Space n) // TranslativeCovering.ConvexBody K}
abbrev SymmetricBody (n : ℕ) :=
  {K : Body n // TranslativeCovering.CentrallySymmetric K.val}

noncomputable def translativeSup (n : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ K : Body n, TranslativeCovering.thetaT K.val

noncomputable def symmetricTranslativeSup (n : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ K : SymmetricBody n, TranslativeCovering.thetaT K.val.val

noncomputable def latticeSup (n : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ K : Body n, thetaL K.val

noncomputable def symmetricLatticeSup (n : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ K : SymmetricBody n, thetaL K.val.val

/-- The finite real comparison scale, viewed in extended nonnegative reals. -/
def orderBounds (c C : ℝ) (n : ℕ) (a : ℝ≥0∞) : Prop :=
  ENNReal.ofReal (c * (n : ℝ) * Real.log (n : ℝ)) ≤ a ∧
    a ≤ ENNReal.ofReal (C * (n : ℝ) * Real.log (n : ℝ))

/-- All four extremal quantities have common
absolute positive comparison constants. ENNReal bounds imply finiteness rather
than mapping a potentially infinite density to zero through `toReal`. -/
def OptimalOrder : Prop :=
  ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n →
    orderBounds c C n (translativeSup n) ∧
    orderBounds c C n (symmetricTranslativeSup n) ∧
    orderBounds c C n (latticeSup n) ∧
    orderBounds c C n (symmetricLatticeSup n)

end CoveringOrder

/-- Translative and lattice covering densities over all convex bodies and over
centrally symmetric convex bodies have the same asymptotic order. -/
theorem CoveringOrder.optimal_order : CoveringOrder.OptimalOrder := by
  sorry

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every sufficiently large dimension <i>n</i>, we construct a centrally symmetric convex body whose translative covering density exceeds $`c n\log n`$, where <i>c</i> &gt; 0 is absolute. This disproves the existence of a universal linear upper bound and matches the order of Rogers' upper bound.

| [stmt-lem-volume](#stmt-lem-volume) | lemma | `lem:volume` | 2 | The body in [eq:body] satisfies |

| [stmt-lem-caps](#stmt-lem-caps) | lemma | `lem:caps` | 4 | Fix $10$. There exist constants $C_a,c_a'>0$, $C_{a,A}>0$, and $L=L(a)>0$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $n$, depending on $a,A,Q,B_0$. |

| [stmt-lem-cylinder](#stmt-lem-cylinder) | lemma | `lem:cylinder` | 11 | Let $n\ge2$, let $D,L>0$, and let $G\subset B_D$ be measurable with $&#124;G&#124;\ge\omega_nD^n/4$. |
